# Engine Research Log

Purpose: keep brief evidence trails for engine verification batches and avoid repeating the same weak source searches. Long page text is intentionally omitted.


## 2026-10-06 - Engine Verified Batch 11 implemented checkpoint: Phase C complete (100 / 125; superseded by final register below)

This preserved intermediate checkpoint documents the first 100 records. The final 125-record register and reconciliation later in this file supersede its status and next-step notes.

- Original 14 drafts: **10 implemented, 4 evidence holds**.
- Qualified replacements immediately implemented: **90**. Qualified reserve remaining: **5**.
- Dataset: **891 total, 802 verified / official, 0 corroborated, 89 legacyPending**.
- Implemented maker breakdown: Nissan=1, Subaru=35, Honda=16, Acura=8, Daihatsu=17, Mazda=9, Isuzu=8, Mitsubishi=6. Markets: Mexico=1, Japan=64, United States=24, Thailand=11. Distinct exact codes: 53. Evidence paths: 76 direct single-source records and 24 direct-identity plus bound supplemental records. All additions have regionKey japan under the existing manufacturer/family-origin convention.
- Original 791 records retain IDs, codes, aliases, text, specifications, verification/sources and regional order. Preserved baseline SHA-256: `51f99c7647b95cf56ef13d931d90c2aa27143969cc93d7fb840544eac85e58d0`. Current 891-record semantic SHA-256: `066323c1361efe064c19840f19945375cc4e579bc714a8041e9400cb78ffd4fe`.

### Implemented records

Every source uses unique IDs, declared field coverage, page notes, normalized exact application/year/market scope and explicit sourceRefs. All are Tier A / official. Supplemental sheets bind directly to the accepted identity source; no bridge chains, family aliases, cross-market specification transfers or inferred cc. When Acura publishes liters only, that precision is retained. vPIC partial model-code patterns use an explicit model year, with only intentional ErrorCode 6 (incomplete VIN) accepted; corrected/ambiguous results are held. Empty Turbo fields never establish naturally aspirated induction. NHTSA evidence is the public manufacturer Part 565 filing, not secondary VIN summaries. All performance figures are ICE engine output, including NSX hybrid applications.

Additional researched leads are not qualified: CR-V VI L15BE (2023) lacks an explicit 16-valve statement in the accepted exact application evidence; Pilot IV J35Y8 / Passport J35Y6 / Ridgeline J35Y6 require exact-scope explicit aspiration; Ridgeline 2023 pattern 5FPYK3F2*P returns correction/multiple-match errors and is not accepted. Integra Type S pattern 19UDE5G4*R does not decode an identity. ADX 2025 is held because the preproduction Acura engine article calls it L15BE while the production-year vPIC pattern reports L15CP; a production manufacturer code statement is still needed to resolve the conflict. Older TL/CL/TSX leads have identity evidence but incomplete aspiration/injection/performance coverage. No specs were fabricated to close these gates.

Other market holds remain in the historical research register: Brazil/Argentina flex-fuel variants lack complete fuel-specific exact calibration fields; Thai/Malaysia candidates need explicit model-year coverage. Tier B remains allowed, but no independent qualifying pair was established this session. No low-readiness catalogue rows were added to the current register.

### Rejected duplicates

The earlier Subaru EA82 catalogue duplicate remains rejected. Equal EG33 grades/years are consolidated into the single implemented 1995 specification snapshot. NP300 YD25DDTi is an evidence hold against the unresolved baseline aggregate and is not counted as a new unique calibration. Three Triton 4N16 calibration drafts and the Destinator 4B40 draft were removed because their codes were already owned by baseline records; they were replaced with unique official Daihatsu scopes. No annual/body/trim/drivetrain-only copies were added. Repeated owners are accepted only for a different model/generation, valvetrain/aspiration, or published power/torque/rpm calibration. The table-driven semantic signature ignores year, trim, transmission, drivetrain and body labels.

### Draft uniqueness and year holds

The temporary draft ID set has no collision with baseline, and normalized exact code + base application + market + full technical/performance signature has no draft duplicate when years and trim/body names are omitted. This lightweight check is not a complete baseline semantic audit.

Baseline exact-code/alias owner review found nissan-yd25ddti and toyota-1gd-ftv. The existing YD25DDTi legacy aggregate names NP300 and spans 2005-2020 but has no normalized performance; the proposed D22 Mexico scope therefore needs an explicit overlap decision and must not be counted as approved unique. The baseline Toyota 2015 launch has 130 kW / 450 Nm, materially different from the Malaysia draft calibrations; neither its market caveat nor a 2026/sept URL directory proves the new drafts' production/model years.

Honda Civic standard L15B7 and hatchback L15BA have equal performance but different documented exact codes. The three L15BA scopes differ by power or power/torque rpm: 174 hp CVT, 180 hp CVT and 180 hp 6MT; gearbox alone is not the justification. L15B7 standard versus Si has different power/torque; L15BE CR-V versus Accord has different application and performance. Body/trim duplicates are consolidated, including Si sedan/coupe and NP300 body/drive columns. No adjacent-year copy was added. The held LX 6MT has 174 hp at 5500 rpm / 167 lb-ft in the 2017 sheet, conflicting with the 2024 overview's 6000 rpm / 162 lb-ft; it is excluded.

Final generation labels below remain proposed metadata until documentary application/generation review; publication snapshots must not be silently promoted to model-year intervals. No broad identity alias or duplicate allow-list is proposed.

### Individual draft evidence register

These IDs and values are proposals, not records present in source JSON. All use provisional regionKey japan, no new aliases, and proposed official / Tier A evidence. DOHC total cams are derived only from the exact-variant head configuration, not a family assumption. Mechanical hp uses 0.7456998716 kW, metric PS/CV uses 0.73549875 kW and lb-ft uses 1.3558179483 Nm, rounded to one decimal. Annual sheets describe only their quoted snapshot; continuing intervals require additional evidence.

## 2026-10-05 - Engine Verified Batch 10 (reconciled)

The blocking duplicate audit reduced the original draft to 18 independently scoped survivors, then added 8 independently researched replacements. The original 765-record baseline remains unchanged. Result: 791 records, 702 verified and 89 legacyPending. Regional additions are North America +17 and Japan +9; Europe, Korea and South America are unchanged. Baseline semantic hash: `df242a63c06cad119811c381ce930da3652dc62d0f1dfda1120034ac87af9fe1`; reconciled result semantic hash: `51f99c7647b95cf56ef13d931d90c2aa27143969cc93d7fb840544eac85e58d0`.

All 26 final records use `evidenceBasis: official`, Tier A manufacturer evidence and a direct `exactVariant` code path. There are no corroborated, Tier B/Tier C or identity-bridge records. Nissan contributes 14 records, INFINITI 3 and Subaru 9. The retained records represent different base models or materially different calibrations; trim, transmission, drivetrain, body style and consecutive press-kit years do not create records.

| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 100 | 18 | 44 | 38 | 0 | 8 | 26 |

The 44 merged entries were redundant trim/year/drivetrain/transmission rows consolidated into retained model/calibration scopes. The 38 baseline overlaps were Mazda SH-VPTR (13), Suzuki R06A (22), Toyota 2ZR-FXE (1), Mitsubishi 4N16 (1) and Hyundai G4FT (1). Accepted replacements are Nissan Frontier VQ38, Pathfinder VQ35DD standard and Rock Creek calibrations, Armada VR35DDTT, TITAN VK56VD, GT-R VR38DETT standard and NISMO calibrations, and INFINITI QX55 KR20DDET.

Additional official candidates were held rather than inferred: the 2025 Murano sheet omits fuel-delivery topology; the checked 2024 QX50/QX55 regional overview pages do not expose a complete exact-code specification table; the checked QX60 regional page omits the internal code and valvetrain details. Repeating annual GT-R, Frontier, QX80 or other unchanged press kits was rejected. The remaining gap to 100 could not be closed without weakening exact-code, scope, valve/camshaft or complete performance evidence requirements.

## 2026-10-04 - Engine Verified Batch 09

Added 100 new application-scoped records without changing or reordering the 665-record baseline. Result: 765 records, 676 verified and 89 legacyPending. Regional changes are Japan +99 and Korea +1; Europe, North America and South America are unchanged. Baseline semantic hash: `b9be450558bdb439f60fa67c1d338c565144559aa8653f8623ff9d505a96b16d`; result semantic hash: `df242a63c06cad119811c381ce930da3652dc62d0f1dfda1120034ac87af9fe1`.

The batch uses eight stored manufacturer groups and no Mercedes-Benz reserve: Toyota 20, Lexus 20, Subaru 15, Suzuki 15, Honda 14, Mazda 8, Mitsubishi 7 and Hyundai Motor Company 1. Every record is `verified` with `evidenceBasis: official`, direct `exactVariant` code binding and Tier A manufacturer evidence. No Tier B corroboration, Tier C closure, identity bridge, family scope or aggregate range is used. Repeated engine codes are retained only as separate named model/grade/year applications; aliases remain unique and the source scope equals the record scope.

Scope decisions and exclusions:

- The requested non-Europe target is exceeded: all 100 additions are outside Europe. North America was left unchanged because the already researched Chevrolet Performance set overlaps Batch 08 identities, while the remaining candidates did not provide a cleaner direct exact-code application than the selected Asian manufacturer tables.
- Toyota and Lexus remain separate stored maker labels, consistent with the catalogue navigation. Neither exceeds 20 records, well below the requested cap of 40 per group.
- Existing broad or earlier application records with the same code are untouched. Each new row names one source-listed application, one year and one market so exact-code search can return all documented owners without collapsing calibrations.
- Block material, timing drive, oil fill, consumption, recurring faults and service life are not supplied by the selected specification evidence and remain explicitly unclaimed.
- Deferred: Nissan and Kia candidates whose official product material did not bind the internal code to the complete power/torque/rpm table; Chevrolet crate-engine pages already represented by the prior batch; any source that exposed only PS/kg-m without an unambiguous manufacturer conversion context.

## 2026-10-02 - Verification hierarchy and Engine Verified Batch 08

The strict policy now supports document-level Tier A/B/C evidence without migrating the original 565 records. New tiered records declare `verification.evidenceBasis`: `official` requires Tier A coverage for every mandatory field; `corroborated` allows a missing Tier A field only when two independent Tier B publishers with different domains and upstream data origins agree for the same exact variant. Tier C is supporting only. Tier B performance coverage also requires structured claims matching the stored values. The policy continues to require a direct exact-code identity source or a single-level exact identity bridge and rejects scope conflicts, bridge chains, aggregate/family-only identity, undeclared valve/camshaft coverage and conflicting performance claims.

Added 100 new records: Mercedes-Benz 96, Mazda 3 and Toyota 1. Regions: Europe +96 and Japan +4. All 100 are `verified`, use `evidenceBasis: official`, direct `exactVariant` code binding and Tier A manufacturer evidence; corroborated records: 0; identity bridges: 0. Source documents: Tier A 101, Tier B 0, Tier C 0. The three Mazda applications and Toyota GR Yaris prevent the batch from being formed entirely from the Mercedes-Benz archive.

Baseline: 565 records, 476 verified, 89 legacyPending; Europe 249, Japan 179, Korea 49, North America 88, South America 0; semantic hash `5424d6844589448b0dc7f771f7613c5b80a172f0018b03ac1048cc78ababbf62`. Result: 665 records, 576 verified, 89 legacyPending; Europe 345, Japan 183, Korea 49, North America 88, South America 0; semantic hash `b9be450558bdb439f60fa67c1d338c565144559aa8653f8623ff9d505a96b16d`.

Mercedes-Benz archive records use `Unspecified` market because the cards do not name a sales market. This means only the exact archived specification, never Global, EU or Germany inferred from archive language or manufacturer origin. Their stored production-calendar intervals include only complete calendar years inside the published month boundaries; exact months remain in each evidence note. All 13 otherwise complete cards whose titles said “only for export” were excluded because the export market was unnamed. Mazda and Toyota records use explicit Japan launch scope. No gearbox, market, period or calibration alternatives were merged into a free range.

Repeated-code audit: repeated Mercedes codes are accepted only for the separately named body type, production-calendar interval and archive card stored in each application; exact-code search returns every owner. The three new `L3-VDT` records remain separate from the existing Mazdaspeed Atenza record and from each other: MPV 23T (180 kW/350 N·m), Mazdaspeed Axela (194 kW/380 N·m) and CX-7 (175 kW/350 N·m). `G16E-GTS` remains separate from the existing 2024 Lexus LBX MORIZO RR calibration: the new record is the 2020 Japan GR Yaris RZ/RC specification at 200 kW/370 N·m. New aliases are application-qualified and collision-free. Regression locks the exact 100-ID order and the complete allowed repeated-code owner map by SHA-256.

Holds and exclusions:

- 13 Mercedes-Benz archive cards with “only for export” but no named market were excluded rather than assigning Global, EU, Germany or an invented export market.
- M133.980 cards that publish a later power/torque change inside one card were excluded; only the A 45 card with a single 265 kW/450 N·m calibration and a safely narrowed 2014 interval was accepted.
- Naturally aspirated Mercedes-Benz cards remain held when induction would be inferred only from the absence of turbocharger or supercharger wording.
- M177/M260/M264/M256/OM654/OM626 searches did not yield complete exact technical cards in the checked archive result set; unrelated body-series matches were rejected.
- Honda E07Z/S07B and Nissan Heritage one-letter-code candidates retain the earlier injection or valve/camshaft coverage gaps. Hyundai/Kia Smartstream candidates remain held at collision-safe exact internal-code binding.

Policy regression grew from the existing 62 fixtures to 79 without rewriting the original cases. The 17 additions cover the five required positive Tier A/B and identity-bridge scenarios plus the required failures: single/same-publisher/shared-origin Tier B evidence, scope mismatches, family identity, performance conflict, missing valve/camshaft coverage, notes without declared fields, Tier C mandatory coverage and a falsely labelled all-Tier-A `corroborated` record.

## 2026-10-02 - Engine Verified Batch 07d

Added 20 direct exact-code records: 12 Japan-region records (Toyota/Lexus, Mazda and Mitsubishi) plus eight Mercedes-Benz reserve records. Direct/bridge breakdown: 20 direct `exactVariant` official sources, 0 identity bridges. Baseline: 545 records, 456 verified, 89 legacyPending; Europe 241, Japan 167, Korea 49, North America 88, South America 0; semantic hash `b4bc48a819e02a4c679db5e93475c6e09dd485583d3821908a302a16092aff46`. Result: 565 records, 476 verified, 89 legacyPending; Europe 249, Japan 179, Korea 49, North America 88, South America 0; semantic hash `5424d6844589448b0dc7f771f7613c5b80a172f0018b03ac1048cc78ababbf62`.

Official evidence: Toyota/Lexus UK launch releases and technical PDFs, Mazda Japan launch/specification tables, Mitsubishi official launch documents/environment report, and Mercedes-Benz Public Archive exact vehicle cards. Market scope is explicit for the 12 Japan-region additions. Mercedes archive cards do not state a sales market, so those eight records use `Unspecified`; every evidence note says this represents only the archived specification and does not claim worldwide applicability. Inline, boxer and V-engine evidence notes explicitly derive total valves and total camshafts; the OM642 total is four (two per bank).

`2UR-GSE` is an intentional repeated code, not an alias collision: the existing record covers Japan 2007/2019 and North America 2016 calibrations, while the new exact record is the separately documented UK 2022 model-year RC F calibration (341 kW/520 Nm, 4,969 cc). Exact-code search returns both records, and the regression permits only these two named owners; the new application alias remains unique.

Holds: Mitsubishi Lancer Evolution VII `4G63` (the official 2001 report extraction labels the table cell `4G93`, so no silent correction); Mazda `SH-VPTS` (an existing record already owns the exact code/application, so a redundant duplicate was rejected); Mercedes `M272.982` and Mazda `PE-VPS` (exact cards do not explicitly state naturally aspirated induction); Honda `E07Z` / `S07B` turbo (official tables still do not establish a compatible injection topology).

Regression coverage reconstructs and hashes the original 545 records in regional order; checks all 20 new IDs, 12/8 diversity, strict sourceRefs, exact-code search, collisions, total valves/camshafts, 4N16 high-output scope, OM651.924 combustion-engine-only performance, region totals and the full semantic hash.

## 2026-10-01 - Engine Verified Batch 07c

Added 20 exact Mercedes-Benz production records from the 525-record baseline. All 20 use strict `sourceRefs` and one direct `exactVariant` manufacturer card; direct/bridge breakdown: 20 direct, 0 identity bridges. Each card binds the exact engine code to the named design/type and covers displacement, cylinder arrangement, valves, camshafts, fuel, aspiration, injection, power and torque with their engine speeds. No values from neighbouring codes, gearboxes, markets or calibrations were combined.

The cards publish production months rather than model years, so the stored year ranges are production-calendar scopes and the exact month boundaries remain in each record's evidence notes. None of the cards states a sales market or worldwide applicability. Both record and source scopes therefore use `Unspecified`, meaning only the exact archived specification and not Global, EU, Germany or any region inferred from the archive language or manufacturer origin.

Construction audit: all inline DOHC cards explicitly publish two overhead camshafts total. M266.980 explicitly publishes one overhead camshaft total. The V8/V12 cards publish one overhead camshaft per cylinder bank; with two banks, the user-facing total is two and the layout is `SOHC per bank`, not one camshaft for the complete engine. Valve totals are derived only from the published cylinders and valves-per-cylinder values, and every source note records that arithmetic.

Holds and exclusions:

- Honda `E07Z / Z Turbo` and `S07B / N-BOX Custom Turbo` — official specifications close exact code, application, displacement, construction, turbocharging and performance, but identify injection only as electronic PGM-FI without an exact injection topology compatible with the present taxonomy. Held rather than storing an inferred multipoint/direct value or `notSpecified` for a mandatory Batch 07c field.
- Hyundai/Kia `G4NIII`, `G4FIII` and related Smartstream brochure candidates — the official material uses commercial engine-family labels rather than collision-safe exact internal codes. Held at exact-code binding.
- Nissan Heritage one-letter `E`, `H`, `J`, `K` and `P` candidates — the official cards still lack explicit total-valve, total-camshaft and injection coverage.
- Mercedes-Benz `OM642.910`, `OM642.920` and `OM642.921` — exact official cards say one overhead camshaft per bank, conflicting with the official OM642.931 construction card used in Batch 07b. Held pending exact-variant construction reconciliation.
- Mercedes-Benz E 400/E 420 CDI archive candidates — the checked cards describe 3,996 cc engines as inline five-cylinder units while also using per-bank camshaft language. Held because the official construction description is internally inconsistent.
- Naturally aspirated Mercedes candidates remain held where aspiration would be inferred only from the absence of turbocharger/supercharger wording.

Regression coverage reconstructs and hashes the original 525 records in regional order, checks all 20 new IDs, strict direct-source policy, exact-code ranking, identity collisions, `Unspecified` scope notes, valve/camshaft totals, the narrowed M113.992 date condition, region totals and the full semantic hash.

Construction audit: the inline cards explicitly state their total overhead-camshaft count. For V engines, the cards state valves per cylinder and camshafts per cylinder bank; evidence notes preserve the arithmetic used for the user-facing totals. SOHC on the M113/M275 cards means one camshaft on each of two banks, not one camshaft for the whole engine. DOHC on OM642/OM629 means two per bank, four total.

Holds and exclusions:

- `OM642.910 / C 320 CDI` — the exact official card publishes gearbox-dependent torque (510 N·m at 1,600-2,800 rpm for the optional automatic, 415 N·m at 1,400-3,800 rpm for the standard manual), but its valve-operation field says one overhead camshaft per bank while the later exact OM642.931 official card says two per bank. Held pending exact-variant construction reconciliation; no free torque range or camshaft assumption was stored.
- Hyundai Smartstream G2.0 MPI, G1.6 T-GDI and G2.5 T-GDI brochure candidates remain on hold because the official sheets do not bind the commercial family labels to collision-safe exact internal engine codes.
- Nissan Heritage one-letter `E`, `H`, `J`, `K` and `P` candidates remain on hold: their cards provide exact vehicle performance but the checked official evidence does not close the newly mandatory total-valve, total-camshaft and injection fields.
- `M111.946`, `M112.947` and `M104.945` remain on hold because aspiration would still be inferred only from the absence of forced-induction wording.

Regression coverage preserves both the original 500-record baseline and the complete 510-record Batch 07b baseline, and checks the 15 IDs, strict direct-source policy, exact-code ranking, identity collisions, valve/camshaft totals, `indirectInjection` translation coverage, the gearbox-qualified OM612.990 torque, the time-qualified OM642.931 torque band, region totals and the full semantic hash.

## 2026-09-30 - Engine Verified Batch 07a

Added 10 exact Mercedes-Benz production records from the 500-record baseline. All 10 use the strict `sourceRefs` format and the direct-code path: the cited manufacturer archive card binds the normalized exact code to one application and standard-production interval and covers every required identity, specification and performance field. Pre-production start dates shown separately by the archive are excluded. The cards are not market-qualified, so each narrow record uses one `Global` market scope rather than inventing a regional calibration claim.

Baseline: 500 records, 411 verified, 89 legacyPending; Europe 196, Japan 167, Korea 49, North America 88, South America 0. Baseline semantic hash: `285441e2e4c553d817065f1d10de76cd04299b5266bbfbe1ced83388c3862187`.

Result: 510 records, 421 verified, 89 legacyPending; Europe 206, Japan 167, Korea 49, North America 88, South America 0. Full semantic hash after the final construction/gearbox audit: `42c983d3fb6841f2d1b5ba3c2c15a99e804d6787e0c372ff2501f02f5de9cd3c`.

Final construction audit: every I4/I5 card explicitly states two overhead camshafts, so the user-facing `layout` now says `DOHC` and `2 camshafts total`. Both M112 V6 cards state one overhead camshaft per cylinder bank; with two banks this is two camshafts total, so the user-facing value now says `SOHC per bank · 18 valves · 2 camshafts total`. Valve totals are derived only from the official per-cylinder counts and cylinder layouts, and that derivation is recorded in each source's evidence notes.

Holds and exclusions:

- `OM605.962` — [E 250 Turbodiesel archive card](https://mercedes-benz-publicarchive.com/marsClassic/en/instance/ko/x.xhtml?oid=5321) verifies the code, application, 1997-1999 standard-production interval, 2,497 cc I5 DOHC 20V, turbocharger, indirect injection, 110 kW at 5,000 rpm and 280 N·m at 1,800-3,600 rpm. Held because the current UI data taxonomy has no exact `indirectInjection` key; using `directInjection`, `commonRail` or `notSpecified` would be incorrect or incomplete, and Batch 07a does not authorize a UI/schema taxonomy change.
- `M111.946`, `M112.947` and `M104.945` — official cards cover code, application and technical data, but naturally aspirated would have to be derived from the absence of forced-induction wording. Held rather than making that inference.
- `D4HB` — not added because exact record `hyundai-d4hb` already exists; no duplicate was created.
- `OM606.962` — excluded as requested; the archive's 210 N·m figure remains subject to separate reconciliation.

Regression coverage added to `tests/engines/engine-search-ui.test.mjs`: expected IDs and uniqueness, strict policy for every new record, exact-code search ranking, code/alias collision rejection, audited cylinder/valve/camshaft layouts, the narrowed OM612.962 gearbox/torque condition, regional totals, complete dataset semantic hash, and reconstruction of the original 500 records with the unchanged baseline semantic hash.

## 2026-09-13 - Engine catalog expansion batch 06

Added 100 verified production records from the synchronized 400-record baseline: Europe +80 (Volvo +40, Mercedes-Benz +40) and Korea +20 (Hyundai Motor Company +20). No crate, competition, racing, test or prototype record was added. Existing records were not edited.

Hyundai brochures publish PS and kgf·m. Stored values use nearest-integer conversions with 1 PS = 0.73549875 kW and 1 kgf·m = 9.80665 N·m; the converted values are not represented as directly published SI data. Volvo and Mercedes-Benz sources publish kW and N·m directly. Mild-hybrid Volvo records store only the combustion-engine Output column and exclude separately listed max-rated values. Volvo states that not all engines are available in all markets; the en-SA, en-KW, en-BH and en-OM support-page locales record where the tables were accessed and are not treated as evidence that a listed engine was sold in that country.

### Batch-06 rejected or deferred candidates

- hyundai-d6cc38: deferred because the official Heavy Duty Truck brochure publishes code-specific power and torque but the checked model table does not tie D6CC38 to one concrete truck model/version.
- hyundai-d4db14: rejected because the Mighty brochure labels the 155 PS output `Gen`, leaving the intended product/application ambiguous.
- mercedes-om606-962: deferred because the official archive page publishes a torque figure that conflicts with the expected model specification and no second official document was used to resolve it.
- Duplicate Mercedes-Benz engine codes encountered on estate/4MATIC pages were rejected rather than added as separate records.
- Brochures or pages without an exact engine code, both performance values, or both rpm values were rejected.

Final audit corrections:

- Corrected `nissan-hr15de` and `nissan-ca18det` to internally consistent official Nissan specifications; IDs remain unchanged and no records were added or removed.
- Documented the `nissan-h20` page's 68 kW / 92 hp rounding conflict while retaining the directly published 68 kW value.
- Restored the complete official Ford product wording in aliases, applications, localized construction text, source titles and source notes for M-6007-572DF, M-6007-572DR, M-6007-M50D, M-6007-M50DAUTO, M-6007-MZ73, M-6007-X2302E, M-6007-Z2363FT, M-6007-Z2363RT, M-6007-Z2427FFT, M-6007-Z2427FRT, M-6007-Z460FFT and M-6007-Z460FRT. Performance and P/N values were unchanged.
- Added the official Ford product pages alongside the instruction sheets for M-6007-X2302E, M-6007-Z2427FFT and M-6007-Z2427FRT so that exact marketed designation/P/N and complete-engine/competition scope are sourced separately from the PDF performance ratings.
- Normalized damaged punctuation in the `nissan-fj20et` source note and removed a duplicated word from three Ford instruction-sheet notes; IDs, applicability and performance values were unchanged.
- Added the schema-required `page: 1` metadata to the three new Ford HTML source entries after the generator rejected the first audit pass; no record data changed.
- Corrective audit removed `nissan-vg20e`: its official page's specification table says VG20E while the descriptive text says VG20ET and attributes the 125 kW / 216 N-m output to that turbocharged version. The source is retained above as `rejected`; the existing separate `nissan-vg20et` was not duplicated or changed.
- Added collision-safe replacement `nissan-ka24de-rev`, restricted to the exact `KA24DE rev.` in the stillborn 1997 Nissan Middle Sports (Z Prototype) S14rev. The official Nissan card directly supplies code, model, 147 kW at 5600 rpm and 256 N-m at 4500 rpm.
- Re-scoped competition/rally/test/prototype records in applications, EN/ES/FR/DE construction text and source notes: `datsun-l24`, `nissan-a12`, `datsun-l18`, `nissan-s20`, `nissan-vr38dett`, `nissan-grx-iii`, `nissan-e15e`, `nissan-lz20b`, `datsun-l14`, `nissan-l20b` and `nissan-ka24de-rev`. Their ratings are no longer presented as whole-family or ordinary road-car values; performance values were unchanged for the ten pre-existing records.
- Automated keyword review also found contextual-only matches in `nissan-fj20et`, `nissan-ma09ert`, `nissan-j20`, `datsun-u20`, `datsun-z18et`, `datsun-z18`, `datsun-l18e`, `datsun-z20e`, `datsun-l13` and `nissan-l16e`. Their source cards specify ordinary road vehicles or the keywords occur only in history/related-information navigation, so no competition scope was inferred.

## 2026-09-09 - Engine catalog expansion batch 04

Added 75 new exact-identity records from the synchronized 225-record main baseline: Korea +4 and Europe +71, for 300 records total. All 75 are `verified`. The original 225 source records remain ahead of the appended regional blocks and are not edited.

The Hyundai G4FT row stores only the combustion-engine rating. The electric-motor and combined-system figures remain in the source note and are explicitly excluded from performance. Mercedes-Benz ranges contain only factory variants named on the same official model page; every boundary is retained in the source note.

Deferred or rejected candidates:

- hyundai-g4kiii / PALISADE Hybrid: deferred because the official sheet prints rpm only for combined output and does not give separate engine-only rpm for both performance fields.
- Kia special-purpose D4DA/D4DD pages: deferred because the checked official pages did not provide both required rpm values.
- Korean brochures that named only a commercial engine family without an exact engine code: rejected for this batch.
- W140 pages for OM603.971, M104.944, M104.990, M104.994, M119.971, M119.970, and M120.980: not added because those exact codes already exist in the synchronized baseline; the pages were used only to reject duplicate candidates.

## 2026-09-08 - Engine catalog expansion batch 03 (finalized 2026-09-09)

Added 50 new exact-identity records in one continuous batch from the synchronized 175-record main baseline: Europe +20 and USA +30, for 225 records total. All 50 are `verified`. The original 175 source records remain ahead of the appended regional blocks and are not edited.

For the Chevrolet Performance rows, the manufacturer publishes mechanical horsepower and pound-feet together with both rpm values. Stored SI values use nearest-integer conversions: 1 mechanical hp = 0.745699872 kW and 1 lb-ft = 1.355817948 N-m. Every source note retains the published imperial figures, exact product name, part number where the page gives one, conversion method, U.S. product-page scope and check date. The Mercedes-Benz archive already publishes kW and N-m.

Identity and scope notes:

- Chevrolet Performance product names are treated as the exact identities: each accepted row has its own marketed designation and, except where the current ZZ427/480 page prints an ambiguous part-number string, its own published part number. No generic LS, Small-Block or Big-Block family ranges are created.
- `LS364/450 Long Block`, `LS9 Long Block` and `LSX376-B15 Long Block` are explicitly limited to the documented U.S. long-block product versions. Their external induction or boost configuration is not generalized to related production engines.
- The 350, 383, 427, 454, 502, 572 and 632 records remain separate only where Chevrolet publishes a distinct product name and performance pair. Equal figures on HT383 and HT383E do not merge the products because the manufacturer supplies separate names and part numbers.
- Every Mercedes-Benz suffix record uses an official model archive that supplies engine type, model designation, production boundary, construction, power, torque and rpm. `M156` uses the official C-Class Estate series page for the AMG 6.3-litre V8 performance pair and a second official image record for the exact M156 code, 6,208 cc displacement and manifold-injection mapping.
- `M104.990` and `M104.994` are separate exact engine types on the same S 320 archive page and are split at the stated June 1993 change. The M119.970, M119.971 and M120.980 ranges contain only the two factory calibrations explicitly bounded before and from September 1992.
- Recurring faults, service-life estimates and oil service fills are not supplied by the selected specification pages and remain unclaimed.

Final review on 2026-09-09:

- Rechecked the published performance pairs against the manufacturer archive and product pages, including date-bounded Mercedes calibrations and Chevrolet hp/lb-ft conversions. The original source-check dates remain recorded with the entries.
- Completed the Spanish, French and German construction descriptions for the new records and corrected the S 55 AMG chassis-type boundary: 220.075 before August 2000, then 220.073. Mercedes-Benz complete-vehicle marketing starts separately in September 2000.
- Normalized indentation in the appended USA source block and regenerated the browser data through `npm run engines:generate`.
- `npm run engines:check` and `npm run engines:legacy-compare` pass. Final regional counts are Europe 45, Japan 87, Korea 25 and USA 68; verification totals are 136 verified and 89 legacyPending.
- Interactive browser review was unavailable because no browser was connected. The generated-data smoke and semantic compatibility checks passed; visual layout was not reviewed in this continuation.

Deferred or rejected candidates from the same pass:

- Chevrolet Performance `SP502 EFI`: the current official 502 page labels it “Coming Soon” and “Dyno Chart Coming Soon”, so there is no complete power/torque/rpm pair to store.
- Chevrolet Performance `525 RLB`: the official off-road page says it is a CT525 designed for off-road competition and publishes the same ratings; it was left out as a use-specific near-duplicate of the accepted CT525 identity.
- Chevrolet Performance `L8T`, `LT4`, `LS3` and `LSX454`: excluded because those identities already exist in the 175-record baseline.
- Mercedes-Benz `OM651.924` in the E 300 BlueTEC HYBRID archive: excluded because the baseline already contains the broader OM651 identity; the combustion engine and hybrid-system figures were not mixed.
- Kia Sportage Smartstream G2.0 / G1.6 T-GDI / D2.0, Kia Niro 1.6 Smartstream GDI, Genesis GV80 G3.5 FR T-GDi and Hyundai Venue Kappa 1.0 T-GDi / U2 1.5 CRDi: official brochures provide performance pairs, but this pass did not find a same-source exact internal engine-code mapping sufficient to rule out overlap with existing Korean code records. They remain candidates rather than being added under inferred codes.
- The batch reaches the requested 50 new records. The deferred candidates remain excluded because they are duplicates, near-duplicates or lack the exact code-to-performance link required by the project policy.

Identity and scope notes:

- 3ZR-FAE remains distinct from the existing 3ZR-FE: the manufacturer tables give separate code rows, grades and ratings.
- 2AZ-FXE and LEB store combustion-engine output and torque only. Toyota's 140 kW hybrid-system output, Honda's 101 kW system output, and all electric-motor figures are excluded.
- C30A and C32B share one NSX table but remain separate identities and transmission/application columns. The same column discipline is applied to J25A/J32A, F23A/J30A, and the three Fit engines.
- The H22A ranges are envelopes of two discrete documented versions: SiR 4AT and S spec 5MT. They do not describe a continuous rating or every H22A.
- Recurring faults, oil service fills, block material and service-life estimates are not supplied by the selected sources and remain unclaimed.
- Honda Civic B16A, D13B and D15B, and Accord F18B/F20B were checked during candidate selection. The older archive tables available in this pass express performance in PS/kg-m; they were not needed because 15 other unique codes had direct official kW/N-m tables. They are left for a future batch rather than introducing derived values here.
- The Toyota Harrier history page was useful for a 1MZ-FE application lead but did not provide the complete performance pair. The Alphard G/V launch table supplies code, power and torque for the stored 1MZ-FE specification.

Honda LEA follow-up: the official Honda tables identify a 1,496 cc SOHC IMA engine paired with MF6. The existing `gm-laf-lea` card describes a General Motors 2.4-litre DOHC engine. The shared `LEA` token therefore does not establish a duplicate; `honda-lea` has its own manufacturer, ID, applications and source trail. Its range includes Fit Hybrid RS (84 kW / 144 N-m) and CR-Z CVT (87 kW / 144 N-m) and 6MT (88 kW / 145 N-m). Electric-motor and combined-system ratings are excluded.

The Toyota 75 Years pages supplement layout and identity only; performance verification relies on the launch releases with both power and torque. Unspecified materials, service intervals, oil capacities, recurring faults and service life remain unclaimed.

## 2026-10-06 - Engine Verified Batch 11 final (125 / 125)

The final phase adds 25 readiness-qualified scopes over the preserved 100-record checkpoint. Dataset reconciliation: 791 baseline + 100 checkpoint + 25 final-phase = 916 records; 827 verified / official, 0 corroborated, 89 legacyPending. Phase A=25, Phase B=25, Phase C=50, final phase=25. Initial research produced 40 final-phase leads; 25 were accepted, 5 remained qualified reserve, and 10 were held or rejected for incomplete valve/induction/year coverage or overlap.

All final-phase records use Tier A official evidence. Eighteen are direct records and seven use an exact application identity plus one bound supplemental construction source. The regional K15 records preserve the exact regional performance table and use the K15 exact-code construction page only for explicitly stated induction/camshaft hardware. ICE outputs are kept separate from hybrid system outputs.

### Final repeated-code audit

- K15B (5): Ertiga Indonesia, XL7 Indonesia, Jimny Indonesia, Jimny 5-Door Philippines and XL6 South Africa are separate base-model/market scopes. Jimny calibrations differ (75 kW Indonesia versus 74.3 kW Philippines); equal trims, transmissions and drivetrains are consolidated.
- K15C (5): four distinct India base models share the manufacturer-published 75.8 kW calibration; South Africa Across is a separate regional base model and 76 kW/137 N·m calibration. Hybrid system outputs are excluded.
- K10C (2): Baleno and Xbee are separate base models with materially different 82/160 and 73/150 calibrations.
- HE-EG (3 Batch 11 owners total): Pyzar is a distinct base model; the 1994 and 1997 Charade Social specifications publish materially different 71/125.5 and 74/127.5 calibrations.
- JB-DET (2 Batch 11 owners total): Copen and Move Custom RS are distinct base models and official application scopes.
- 3B20 (2): one base model has explicit naturally aspirated and turbo columns with materially different induction and performance; grades/drivetrains are consolidated.

### Final reserve and holds

Qualified reserve retained outside the fixture: Daihatsu Atrai EF-TS (explicit valve count still needs an exact official row), Daihatsu Delta Wagon 3C-T (exact valve count still needs closure), HINO300 N04C-UL (complete ICE performance/rpm not in the exhibit row), HINO Dakar J08C-TI (complete performance/rpm not in the exhibit row), and Suzuki Fronx K15C Japan (FWD/AWD calibration split needs one exact scope selection). Other rejected leads were K15B-C Carry and K14B Baleno regional rows with incomplete explicit induction coverage, plus same-calibration trim/drivetrain candidates. None is implemented or included in the fixture.

### Final reconciliation

- Initial final-phase leads: 40. Accepted final-phase additions: 25. Qualified reserve: 5. Holds/rejections: 10.
- Original drafts: 10 accepted / 4 holds. Earlier qualified replacements: 90. Final phase: 25. Batch 11 total: 125.
- Baseline overlaps rejected before implementation; no broad aliases or allow-list entries added.
- Market-diversification target met: 10 final-phase records outside Japan and United States.
